:PROJECT:      theaspirational.slack-mcpl
:TASK:         TASK-0901Y
:REASON:       The fork's requireWritableConversation performs a fresh conversations.info (or conversations.history) Slack call before it can confirm membership and scopes. This conflicts with requiring both the complete writability check and text validation before any Slack API call. Should the merge (A) preserve full fork writability-refusal precedence, then validate text before any subsequent DM/open/write call, allowing only the existing authorization lookup first; or (B) validate text after local allow-list/scope checks but before the fresh lookup, so malformed text takes precedence over membership refusals?
:RUN_ID:       run-01M618AXR5KZJTWNW7HNHPNF0Z
:ASK_ID:       ask-e1aac0460e2c4bc6b4ff1161703030e8
:TIMEOUT_SECS: 1800
:EVENT_ID:     2071abde-1ef7-49db-9a0b-e23c73b16ad9
:END:

* TX 2026-10-28 Wed 12:40:27 run.answered TASK-0901Y
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261028-theaspirationalslack-188b40d8-3166-4a1e-a1eb-e81578e835d0

:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.slack-mcpl
:TASK:         TASK-0901Y
:REASON:       Choose A. Keep the fork's full writability refusal precedence, including its existing read-only authorization lookup (conversations.info / conversations.history), which carries no message text. Then validate text (upstream's lone-surrogate check) before any subsequent call that carries text or opens/writes (DM open, chat.postMessage, chat.update, etc.). The invariant to preserve is: no Slack call ever carries malformed text; refusal order is allow-list/scope/membership first, then text, matching upstream's documented order. Add a test showing malformed text to a non-writable conversation yields the writability refusal, and malformed text to a writable one yields the text refusal with zero text-carrying Slack calls. State this deviation from the literal brief wording in the report. Please quote this answer's first sentence back in your report.

:ASK_ID:       ask-e1aac0460e2c4bc6b4ff1161703030e8

:TASK:         TASK-2XGBG
:REASON:       Review read in full: "approve with changes", three MEDIUMs. 1) conn nulled after the reset, so a message during teardown is acked and then dropped. 2) awaited reset in serve() finally, so a reconnecting TCP host is refused for a backlog-bounded time. 3) a non-Error rejection in callStatus exits the process and poisons the queue. LOWs: wrong migration link, duplicate post-clear, unreachable identity guards, undeclared op caveat, receipt change undocumented, 25 ms negative asserts. Closing as reject for a fix round.

I'm closing it as a reject so I can run a small fix round for those two MEDIUMs plus the cheap LOWs.

:REASON:       A1 and fork 685b2deb have no Slack channel-join API/tool; conversations.open only serves send_dm, while channels/open is a synchronous server registry operation. The approved A2 plan covers adapter gates and leaves server attachment enforcement to A3. Should I interpret “channel open and join” as gating the existing send_dm open plus fresh listing/metadata admission (no new join API or server channels/open behavior), or do you intend a new server-level open/join gate outside the A2 plan?

:REASON:       Permit it. Uploading or deleting into a named group DM is a write to an admitted conversation, which is exactly what the shared guard means. The parallel change owns *reading* files and attachments (where bytes can come from an unadmitted conversation), not writes into admitted ones.
